## Assistant

Good topic — the doc is large enough that a graph is useful, but deep-links need a small amount of structure work first.

## What the matrix/graph would show

Natural **nodes** (from current inventory, ~70+ `REQ-*` plus OQs):

| Node type | Examples | Useful attributes |
|---|---|---|
| **Requirement** | `REQ-M3`, `REQ-V12` | family (M/V/A…), milestone, status |
| **Open question** | `OQ-1`, `OQ-33` | BLOCKING / OPEN / RESOLVED / DEFERRED |
| **Section** | `§5 Core Mechanism` | cluster container |
| **Milestone** | alpha / beta / final / 2.0 | release gate |

Natural **edges** (parseable from prose if you’re disciplined, or maintained lightly):

| Edge | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `depends_on` | A needs B | `REQ-T5 → REQ-A4` |
| `resolves` | REQ closes OQ | `REQ-M5 → OQ-2` |
| `extends` / `refines` | specialization | `REQ-V12 → REQ-V4` |
| `gates` | release gate | `REQ-QA2 → 1.0 final` |
| `cross_cuts` | global constraint | `REQ-S1 → REQ-V2, REQ-T2, …` |

Hubs you already have by cross-reference count: **REQ-A4, REQ-M3, REQ-S1, REQ-F4, REQ-V5** — those become the graph’s “centers of gravity.”

---

## The hard part: `REQUIREMENTS.md#…` must actually land

GitHub (and most Markdown renderers) only auto-anchor **headings**, not bold list items like:

```markdown
- **REQ-M3 — Screen understanding falls back…**
```

So today:

- `REQUIREMENTS.md#5-core-mechanism` works  
- `REQUIREMENTS.md#req-m3-…` does **not**

### Fix options (pick one)

**A. Explicit HTML anchors next to each REQ (recommended for this repo)**  
```markdown
- <a id="req-m3"></a>**REQ-M3 — Screen understanding…**
```
Stable, short, case-insensitive fragments: `#req-m3`, `#oq-1`.  
Pros: works on GitHub, no renumbering pain. Cons: slight visual noise in source (or put anchors on their own line).

**B. Promote each REQ to a heading**  
```markdown
#### REQ-M3 — Screen understanding…
```
Pros: free GitHub slugs. Cons: TOC becomes huge; slug changes if the title text changes unless you pin IDs.

**C. Generated HTML page** with real `id`s, graph links to that HTML, Markdown stays source of truth.  
Pros: best UX. Cons: second artifact to keep in sync (fine if CI-generated).

**D. Line-number links** (`#L159`)  
Fragile under every edit — skip for a living draft.

**Recommendation:** **A** (or A+C). Stable `#req-m3` IDs match how you already talk about requirements, and graph nodes can hardcode `id = lower(REQ-id)`.

---

## How to render the graph

Three tiers, increasing complexity:

### 1. Static Mermaid / Graphviz in-repo (lightest)
- Script parses `REQUIREMENTS.md` → emits `docs/requirements-graph.md` with Mermaid `flowchart` / `graph`.
- Clickable nodes: Mermaid supports link syntax to `#req-m3`.
- Pros: lives next to the public doc, no server, fits REQ-P3 (requirements repo only).  
- Cons: dense graphs get unreadable; filtering is limited.

### 2. Interactive HTML (best “matrix + graph” experience)
- Build a single static page (e.g. **D3 force graph**, **Cytoscape.js**, **vis-network**, or **Observable**-style).
- Left/side panel = **matrix** (filter by family, status, milestone).
- Main canvas = **graph**; click node → opens `REQUIREMENTS.md#req-m3` (GitHub blob URL) or in-page panel with excerpt.
- Generated from Markdown so the graph never drifts.

Example node payload:
```json
{
  "id": "REQ-M3",
  "family": "M",
  "title": "Screenshot-based recognition…",
  "href": "https://github.com/brianreborn/UMAssisted/blob/master/REQUIREMENTS.md#req-m3",
  "status": "active",
  "section": "5"
}
```

### 3. Matrix-first, graph secondary
A **traceability matrix** table is often more useful day-to-day than a full force graph:

| REQ | Depends on | Resolves | Gates | Status |
|-----|------------|----------|-------|--------|
| REQ-V12 | REQ-V4, REQ-V11 | OQ-30 | — | decided |
| REQ-QA2 | REQ-A10, REQ-V9 | — | 1.0 final | open verify |

Each cell is a link. Graph is a *view* of the same edge list, not a second source of truth.

---

## Architecture that fits this project

```
REQUIREMENTS.md  (canonical prose + stable #req-* anchors)
       │
       ▼ parse (script, CI or manual)
  requirements-graph.json  (nodes + edges)
       │
       ├─► requirements-matrix.md   (tables, GitHub-readable)
       ├─► requirements-graph.mmd   (Mermaid)
       └─► requirements-graph.html  (optional interactive)
```

**Source of truth stays the Markdown** — same rule as REQ-OQ1. Graph/matrix are projections.

**Edge authoring options:**

1. **Inferred** — regex for `REQ-X`, `Resolves OQ-N`, `see REQ-Y` (works today, noisy).  
2. **Structured front-matter per REQ** (cleaner long-term):
   ```markdown
   <!-- req id=M3 depends=M1,S1 resolves=OQ-2 gates= -->
   - <a id="req-m3"></a>**REQ-M3 — …**
   ```
3. **Hybrid** — infer first, maintain only high-value edges (`depends_on`, `gates`) explicitly.

For a living draft, **hybrid** is enough: infer cross-refs for “mentions,” curate `depends_on` / `gates` for the matrix that actually matters.

---

## Design choices worth deciding before building

1. **Audience** — GitHub readers only, or local HTML for you? (Public requirements repo → static artifacts only; no app backend.)
2. **Primary view** — force graph for exploration, or matrix for “what’s blocking what?”
3. **OQs in the graph?** — yes for BLOCKING/OPEN; hide RESOLVED by default or fade them.
4. **Cross-cutting nodes** (`REQ-S1`, `REQ-A5`, `REQ-A11`) — show as hubs, or as filters so they don’t spiderweb everything?
5. **Anchor strategy** — commit to `#req-m3` / `#oq-1` now so links never churn.
6. **Generation** — one Python/Node script checked in vs. manual Mermaid (manual dies quickly at 70+ nodes).

---

## Practical minimal path

1. Add stable anchors: `#req-p1` … `#req-oq3`, `#oq-1` …  
2. Emit `graph.json` from a parse of IDs + “Resolves OQ-*” + explicit `depends` comments.  
3. Ship **matrix MD** first (usable on GitHub immediately).  
4. Add **Mermaid** or a small **static HTML** graph second.

---

## Open product/process question

Should the graph itself become a **requirement** (e.g. REQ-OQ4: “requirements graph/matrix is a generated view of this doc, kept in repo”)? That would fit REQ-OQ1/OQ3’s self-containment story — or stay tooling outside the SRS.

If you want to proceed, useful next forks:

- **A)** Anchor convention only (prep the doc)  
- **B)** Spec the matrix columns + edge types as a small REQ/tooling note  
- **C)** Prototype a parser + Mermaid or HTML from current `REQUIREMENTS.md`

Which of those matches what you had in mind — matrix-first, interactive graph, or both?

## User

maybe it should just be a tree? but I mean it should have arrows connecting things to show the graphness of it all

## Assistant

A **tree layout with graph edges** is a better fit than either a pure tree or a pure force graph.

## The shape

- **Tree (layout / nesting):** where a requirement *lives*  
  `§5 Core Mechanism → REQ-M3 → bullets / residual OQs`
- **Graph (arrows):** how requirements *relate*  
  `REQ-T5 → depends on → REQ-A4`  
  `REQ-M5 → resolves → OQ-2`  
  `REQ-QA2 → gates → 1.0 final`

So: **hierarchical placement + non-tree arrows**. Not “only parent/child,” and not a hairball of free-floating nodes.

```
§6.3 Voice
├── REQ-V1
│   └── REQ-V5  ──────────────┐
│       └── REQ-V6            │  (extends / raises bar)
├── REQ-V4 ◄──────────────────┼── REQ-V12  (confirmation form)
│                             │
└── REQ-V9 ◄── same state ────┘── REQ-V13
         │
         └── always-visible overlay ◄── REQ-A10  (cross-section arrow)
```

Parent lines = document structure.  
Colored/dashed arrows = semantic graph.

---

## Why this works for UMAssisted

| Pure tree | Pure force graph | Tree + arrows |
|---|---|---|
| Loses “REQ-V2 needs REQ-S1” | Loses § structure / reading order | Keeps both |
| Cross-links become fake nesting | Hubs (A4, M3, S1) explode the layout | Cross-links stay as arrows |
| Matches the MD outline | Feels exploratory but hard to scan | Outline = backbone; arrows = load-bearing |

Your doc is **already a tree** (sections → REQs). The interesting part is the **graphness on top** — dependencies, resolves, gates, cross-cuts.

---

## Edge kinds (keep few)

Too many arrow types = spaghetti. Start with 3–4:

| Arrow | Style idea | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **depends on** | solid | must exist / hold for this to work |
| **resolves** | solid, OQ → REQ or REQ → OQ | decision closed |
| **extends / implements** | dashed | specialization of a parent rule |
| **gates** | thick / milestone color | release blocker |

Cross-cutting hubs (`REQ-S1`, `REQ-A5`, `REQ-A11`) should **not** get 40 spokes drawn always — filter them (“show S1 edges”) or draw them as a **side rail** the tree sits under, with optional highlight.

---

## Layout patterns that match “tree but arrows”

1. **Indented outline + arc links** (side or over the tree)  
   Classic “code map” / dependency-on-outline. Best for GitHub + simple HTML.

2. **Tidy tree (Reingold–Tilford) + curved edges for non-parent links**  
   What people expect from “org chart with extra arrows.” D3 `tree` + second edge layer.

3. **Clustered sections as columns**  
   Each § is a column; REQs stacked inside; arrows cross columns. Good for “architecture vs voice vs QA.”

4. **Mermaid `flowchart` with subgraphs**  
   ```text
   subgraph Voice
     V4 --> V12
   end
   S1 --> V2
   ```
   Subgraphs = tree clusters; `-->` = graph. Limited interactivity, fine as a first shippable view.

Recommendation for a first real artifact: **(1) or (2)** — outline backbone you already know, arrows for the graphness.

---

## Interaction (still link into the doc)

- Click **node** → `REQUIREMENTS.md#req-v12` (after stable anchors).
- Click **arrow** → optional tooltip (“depends on: no network → on-device STT only”).
- Toggle layers: `depends` / `resolves` / `gates` / “mentions only.”
- Collapse tree branches (§6.3 closed) while keeping cross-arrows to visible nodes, or dim them.

---

## What *not* to do

- **Fake tree by inventing a single parent for every REQ** — many things legitimately hang off several roots (S1, A11, VAL2). Forced unique parent lies.
- **Draw every textual mention as an arrow** — `REQ-A4` would become unreadable. Curate: structure edges + high-value semantic edges; mentions stay in search/matrix.
- **Replace the MD with the graph** — graph is a view; prose stays canonical (REQ-OQ1).

---

## Minimal conceptual model

```text
Node {
  id: "REQ-V12"
  parent: "§6.3" | "REQ-V4"   // tree placement only (one primary home)
  href: ".../REQUIREMENTS.md#req-v12"
}

Edge {
  from, to
  kind: depends | resolves | extends | gates
  // never used for layout of the tree spine
}
```

**One primary home in the tree** (document section, or “extends X” when that’s the real home).  
**Many arrows** for everything else.

---

## Practical first cut

1. Tree = current heading hierarchy + each `REQ-*` / open `OQ-*` as a leaf under its section.  
2. Arrows = only: `Resolves OQ-N`, explicit “see REQ-X” where X is load-bearing, milestone `gates`.  
3. Stable `#req-v12` anchors so clicks leave the tree into the prose.  
4. Default view: tree expanded to section level; arrows on for `depends` + `resolves`; `S1` cross-cuts off until toggled.

That’s “just a tree” for navigation, with **arrows carrying the graphness** without turning the page into a physics simulation.

If you want to push this into the repo next, natural sequence is: **anchor IDs → edge list (small JSON) → one HTML/Mermaid view with tree spine + arrows**. I can sketch the edge list from a few high-value hubs (S1, M3, A4, V4/V12) first so the shape is concrete before any tooling.

## Assistant

Added **REQ-A13** — **"Don't save that"** for event selections.

### Behavior
- User still picks the in-game option for **this** occurrence.
- UMAssisted **does not** store it as the standing `(support card, event)` answer (and **does not** overwrite an existing one).
- So that pick **cannot** become a future **REQ-A8** auto-replay precedent.

### Details
| Case | Effect |
|---|---|
| No prior recording | Key stays empty; later still falls through |
| Prior recording exists | Prior answer unchanged; auto-replay still uses the old one |
| Surfaces | Voice (compound phrases) + any mediated confirm UI; optional sticky “next: don’t save” |
| Default preference | Fresh install: **save by default**; user can switch to **remember only when asked**; explicit don’t-save/do-save wins per pick |

Cross-linked from **REQ-A4**, **REQ-A8**, and **REQ-A7** settings. Pushed as `f4836ad`.

## Assistant

Added **REQ-A14**.

### Commands
| Phrase (defaults; customizable) | Selects |
|---|---|
| **gamble** | The one option tagged **multi-outcome** |
| **safe** | The one option tagged **single-outcome**, when another option is multi-outcome |

### Rules
- Tags come from **offline corpus** (event data + review), not live “what looks riskier.”
- **Exactly one** matching option → select it; otherwise **fall through** and say so (no guessing).
- Works with **don’t save** (`gamble, don't save`, etc.).
- If saved for auto-replay, stores the **concrete option**, not the word “gamble.”
- Still **user-originated** — never auto-picks safe/gamble on its own (REQ-A11).

**OQ-38** left open for exotic layouts (3+ options, dual gambles, etc.). Pushed as `5254e2a`.

- Execute: cd /home/dev/UMAssisted && /usr/local/bin/python3.12 <<'PY'
import re
from pathlib import Path
from collections import defaultdict, Counter
text = Path('REQUIREMENTS.md').read_text()
lines = text.splitlines()
print('lines', len(lines), 'chars', len(text))

# REQ headers
req_re = re.compile(r'^- \*\*(REQ-[A-Z][A-Z0-9]*)\s*[—–-]\s*(.+?)\*\*', re.M|re.S)
reqs = []
for m in req_re.finditer(text):
    rid = m.group(1)
    title = re.sub(r'\s+',' ', m.group(2))[:80]
    line = text.count('\n',0,m.start())+1
    reqs.append((rid, line, title))

print('\n=== REQ count by family ===')
fam = Counter(re.match(r'REQ-([A-Z]+)', r[0]).group(1) for r in reqs)
for f,c in sorted(fam.items()):
    print(f, c)

print('\n=== Duplicate REQ ids ===')
c = Counter(r[0] for r in reqs)
print([k for k,v in c.items() if v>1] or 'none')

print('\n=== Numeric order issues (family) ===')
by_f = defaultdict(list)
for rid, line, title in reqs:
    m = re.match(r'REQ-([A-Z]+)(\d+)', rid)
    if m:
        by_f[m.group(1)].append((int(m.group(2)), rid, line, title))
for f, items in sorted(by_f.items()):
    nums = [i[0] for i in items]
    if nums != sorted(nums):
        print(f, 'OUT OF ORDER in doc:', [i[1] for i in items])
    # gaps
    if nums:
        missing = [n for n in range(min(nums), max(nums)+1) if n not in nums]
        if missing:
            print(f, 'gaps:', missing, 'have:', sorted(nums))

print('\n=== WITHDRAWN / DEFERRED / stub ===')
for pat in ['WITHDRAWN', 'DEFERRED', 'Stub', 'stub', 'Tentative', 'soft, conditional']:
    for i,l in enumerate(lines,1):
        if pat in l and ('REQ-' in l or '###' in l or 'Open' in l):
            if i < 1800:
                print(f'{i}: {l[:100]}')

print('\n=== Cross-ref density top ===')
all_ids = re.findall(r'REQ-[A-Z][A-Z0-9]*', text)
print(Counter(all_ids).most_common(15))

print('\n=== Repeated structural phrases ===')
phrases = [
    'fall through',
    'user-extensible',
    'REQ-V8/V11',
    'does not expand automation',
    'Does not violate REQ-A11',
    'first-class',
    'never guess',
    'do not invent',
    'single-shot',
    'always-visible',
    'on-device',
    'comprehension',
]
for p in phrases:
    print(f'{text.lower().count(p.lower()):3}  {p}')

print('\n=== Section structure ===')
for i,l in enumerate(lines,1):
    if l.startswith('##'):
        print(f'{i}: {l}')

import re
from pathlib import Path
text = Path('REQUIREMENTS.md').read_text()

# Find multi-form selection patterns (V15, V16, A14, A15)
print('=== Selection form requirements ===')
for m in re.finditer(r'^- \*\*(REQ-[A-Z0-9]+)[^*]+\*\*', text, re.M):

    if any(k in title.lower() for k in ['form', 'gamble', 'energy', 'race', 'option', 'semantic', 'select']):

## Refactor check — golden opportunities

~2200 lines, **~80 REQs**, no duplicate IDs. IDs are **intentionally stable** (doc status line), so renumbering is a *bad* “refactor.” Real wins are **structure, shared patterns, and dead weight**.

---

### Tier 1 — High value, low ID risk

**1. Extract a shared “multi-form selection” pattern**  
You now have the same shape in several places:

| Spec | Domain |
|---|---|
| **REQ-V15** | Event options |
| **REQ-V16** | Race list |
| **REQ-A14** | gamble / safe |
| **REQ-A15** | take the energy |

Repeated machinery: ordinal · on-screen text · semantic role · fall-through if ambiguous · V8/V11 phrases · concrete id for A4/A8 · V4/V12 confirm.

**Refactor:** one short meta-REQ (e.g. under §6.2 or §6.3), e.g. **“list/choice selection forms — shared rules”**, then V15/V16/A14/A15 only state *domain-specific* forms. Cuts a lot of prose and keeps new surfaces (skills, shop) cheap.

**2. Unify “select + begin” compounds under V12**  
Same commit pattern, three spellings:

- `"G1 G1"` → select + begin  
- `"just race the scheduled race"` → open + select + begin  
- V12 repeat-to-confirm generally  

**Refactor:** one rule: *repeat same selection action (or dedicated compound) within confirm window = select+commit when the game has a begin step.* V16 only lists which race pointers support double-begin (grade required; name/default encouraged).

**3. “Standing rules” strip for cross-cuts**  
These are restated constantly: **A5** (no self-loop), **A6** (human speed ceiling), **A11** (no independent decisions), **M3 fall-through**, **S1** (no network), **V4/V12** confirm.

**Refactor:** a tight § (or box at start of §6) “Standing constraints — all features inherit these,” then body REQs **cite** instead of re-explaining. Biggest readability win for agents skimming.

**4. Corpus stack as one picture**  
Scattered: **M3/M5/M6/M7**, **F4** labels, **A14** tags, **A15** energy fields, **A15a** Effects path.

**Refactor:** one subsection “Offline corpus layers” (identity · text · no-choice · gamble/safe · energy · UI templates) + “live Effects fallback.” Implementation and labeling become obvious.

---

### Tier 2 — Medium value (hygiene)

**5. Document order vs numeric order**  
A16 sits before A10; V11 before V9; F5 before F2. Fine for stable IDs, bad for reading.

**Refactor:** reorder *prose* into theme blocks without changing IDs:

- View assist: A9 → A16 → A10  
- Decision memory: A4 → A8 → A14 → A15  
- Voice selection: V15 → A14/A15 refs → V16  

**6. Collapse §9 RESOLVED entries**  
REQ-OQ2 wants history kept, but many RESOLVED blocks are long.

**Refactor:** RESOLVED = one line (question + answer + REQ id); OPEN/BLOCKING keep full text. Cuts registry noise a lot.

**7. Graduate or corral stubs**  
`REQ-S2`, `REQ-SF6`, `REQ-ON1` + awkward **§8.1a**.

**Refactor:** either “§ X Stubs / gap REQs” or flesh ON1 into real first-run flow next to V1. Stops stubs looking like finished design.

**8. Retire A13 more cleanly**  
Withdrawn body still occupies automation § mentally.

**Refactor:** move withdrawn IDs to a short appendix; leave one-line “A13 withdrawn → see A8” in place.

**9. V7 inventory vs V16**  
Races are half-specified in V16 but OQ-22 still says “races in full.”

**Refactor:** V7 inventory = checklist table; V16/A9/… = the real specs; OQ-22 only tracks *unobserved* rows.

---

### Tier 3 — Don’t bother (or do later)

| Temptation | Why not |
|---|---|
| Renumber REQs into perfect order | Breaks “IDs stable”; breaks map, chat, git history |
| Merge A14+A15+V15 into one giant REQ | Becomes unreadable; domains differ |
| Delete all RESOLVED OQs | Loses self-contained history (REQ-OQ2) |
| Split file into many MD files | Hurts single-doc pickup story unless tooling catches up |

---

### Already in decent shape

- **A10 + A16** sharing one sweep toggle — right model  
- **P3** docs-open / app-closed — clear  
- **No duplicate REQ ids**  
- **Map generator** as projection of the doc — good  

---

### Suggested order if you want refactors done

1. Standing-rules strip + cite-don’t-repeat  
2. Shared multi-form selection meta-REQ; slim V15/V16/A14/A15  
3. Double-begin unified under V12; V16 only lists race cases  
4. Corpus layers subsection  
5. Reorder blocks by theme (IDs unchanged)  
6. Collapse RESOLVED OQs to one-liners

## Assistant

Added **REQ-V17** (Aoharu Hai spirit-burst facility pick).

### Selection rules
| Utterance | When it works |
|---|---|
| **"purple"**, **"blue"**, … | Exactly one facility has that burst type |
| **"burst"** / **"spirit burst"** | Exactly one facility has *any* burst |
| Ambiguous (2+ purples, or 2+ any bursts for bare "burst") | Fall through — no guess |

### Other details
- Same action family as saying **"speed"** / **"stamina"** (confirm rules unchanged)
- Only on training UI when spirit-burst chrome is present
- Screen understanding only — not “pick the best burst”
- Works with auto-sweep TTS announcements when detection is solid

**OQ-42**: full color/type inventory on Global. Pushed as `b0d5881`.

- Execute: mkdir -p /home/dev/UMAssisted/screenshots/{hub,races,skills,shop,recreation,infirmary,events,aoharu,misc}
# helper for capture
cat > /home/dev/UMAssisted/tools/capture_screen.sh << 'EOF'
#!/bin/sh
# Passive screenshot: no input injection. Usage: capture_screen.sh <label>
# Saves under screenshots/<subdir or misc>/<timestamp>_<label>.png
set -e
export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"
LABEL="${1:-screen}"
SUB="${2:-misc}"
ROOT="$(CDPATH= cd -- "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"
DIR="${ROOT}/screenshots/${SUB}"
mkdir -p "$DIR"
TS="$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S)"
OUT="${DIR}/${TS}_${LABEL}.png"
REMOTE="/sdcard/uma_cap_${TS}.png"
adb shell screencap -p "$REMOTE"
adb pull "$REMOTE" "$OUT" >/dev/null
adb shell rm -f "$REMOTE" 2>/dev/null || true
# also dump uiautomator (passive) next to it
DUMP="${DIR}/${TS}_${LABEL}.uixml"
adb shell uiautomator dump /sdcard/uma_ui.xml 2>/dev/null || true
adb pull /sdcard/uma_ui.xml "$DUMP" 2>/dev/null || true
adb shell rm -f /sdcard/uma_ui.xml 2>/dev/null || true
echo "saved $OUT"
# quick package check
adb shell dumpsys window 2>/dev/null | grep -E 'mCurrentFocus|mFocusedApp' | head -3
EOF
chmod +x /home/dev/UMAssisted/tools/capture_screen.sh
echo "ready"
 (Create screenshots dirs and capture helper)

- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb devices -l 2>&1 | cat (Confirm the device is connected.)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb shell 'ls -lt /sdcard/Pictures/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -5; echo "----"; ls -lt /sdcard/DCIM/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -5; echo "----"; ls -lt /sdcard/Pictures/ 2>/dev/null | head -3' 2>&1 | cat (Locate the most recent system screenshots on the device (common Android locations).)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb shell 'ls -1t /sdcard/Pictures/Screenshots/*.png /sdcard/DCIM/Screenshots/*.png /sdcard/Screenshots/*.png 2>/dev/null | head -5' 2>&1 | cat; echo '---'; adb shell 'ls -1t /sdcard/Pictures/Screenshots/ /sdcard/DCIM/Screenshots/ /sdcard/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -8' 2>&1 | cat (Locate the two most recent system-taken screenshots on the device.)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb connect 192.168.1.123:38841 2>&1 | cat; sleep 1; adb devices -l 2>&1 | cat (Reconnect wireless adb to the Pixel 8 and confirm it is online.)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb shell 'ls -1t /sdcard/Pictures/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -3; ls -1t /sdcard/DCIM/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -3; ls -1t /sdcard/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -3' 2>&1 | cat (Once reconnected, list the newest system screenshots on the device.)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb disconnect 192.168.1.123:38841 2>/dev/null; sleep 1; adb connect 192.168.1.123:38841 2>&1 | cat; sleep 2; adb devices -l 2>&1 | cat (Force disconnect + reconnect attempt to bring the wireless device back online for pulling the system screenshots.)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb kill-server 2>/dev/null; sleep 0.5; adb start-server 2>/dev/null; sleep 1; adb connect 192.168.1.123:38841 2>&1 | cat; sleep 3; adb devices -l 2>&1 | cat (Kill and restart adb server then reconnect to the wireless device.)
- Execute: export PATH="${HOME}/japanglify/sdk/platform-tools:${PATH}"; adb shell 'ls -1t /sdcard/Pictures/Screenshots/ /sdcard/DCIM/Screenshots/ /sdcard/Screenshots/ 2>/dev/null | head -5' 2>&1 | cat (Once connected, list the newest system screenshots on the device.)

## Assistant

**Done. Review complete + 1.0 alpha plan scaffolded as public docs.**

### 1.0 Alpha Definition (directly from REQUIREMENTS.md §2 + REQ-V7 + REQ-F5)
- **First working build**. Incomplete voice is explicitly **acceptable**.
- Beta hard-blocks on full voice control of *everything inside a career* (REQ-V7) + UI coverage verification progress (REQ-QA1).
- Final hard-blocks on overlay tested against every scenario (REQ-QA2) + security audit (REQ-QA3).

Alpha is deliberately the smallest slice that proves the core value under all the hard constraints (named sequences only, no self-looping, never faster than best-case human, passive-only during dev, no network, AccessibilityService + corpus, REQ-P3 private implementation).

### Hard Must-Haves for Alpha
**Priority order (REQ-F5, alpha-critical):**
1. Training auto-sweep (REQ-A9/A10) — hover Speed/Stamina/Power/Guts/Wit with human-readable dwell. Same toggle also arms list auto-scroll (REQ-A16).
2. No-choice auto-advance (REQ-F2/F4) — dialogue, results, skips, Next/Close via human-labeled generic-UI corpus.
3. Shop browse-through (REQ-A1) — after the above two are usable.

**Core that applies from the first line:**
- `AccessibilityService` + `takeScreenshot()` (REQ-M1/M3). Confirmed: game is opaque `unitySurfaceView`; node tree is dead.
- ML Kit bundled OCR + fuzzy corpus match (REQ-M4/M6). Two layers: event text (local `master.mdb` extract) + generic-UI (hand-labeled screenshots).
- Named sequences only (REQ-A1). Never full autoplay.
- Decision replay is user precedent only (REQ-A4), opt-in per-event (REQ-A8), first occurrence always falls through.
- **REQ-A5 (hard)**: no self-looping. Fresh explicit command every time.
- **REQ-A6 (hard)**: never faster/easier than best-case able human.
- Always-visible overlay kill switches (sweep + voice listening) (REQ-A7/A10/V9).
- Structural no-network (REQ-S1). Local config only.
- Safety/non-interference + foreground package guard (REQ-SF1/3/6).
- Ethics chain during dev (REQ-DEV1/2/3): you perform live actions; tools only observe. Real `dispatchGesture` test code (if ever needed) must be single-button, two-step, tiny, staged, user-watched, and uninstalled.
- REQ-P3 boundary (see below).

**Voice/TTS**: partial is fine. Wake word + basic commands (facilities, "rest", ordinals, on-screen text, gamble/safe/take-the-energy, scheduled race) + confirmation window + some readout. Full in-career parity is beta.

**Validation mindset**: design must stay reconcilable to REQ-VAL2 (no extra capability, no unattended/speed advantage, auditable, same category as TalkBack/Switch Access). The formal pass is required before 1.0 ships.

### Explicitly Not Required for Alpha
- Complete OQ-22 inventory (shop purchase, full Skills, full Races beyond entry/scheduled, Recreation, Infirmary, hamburger contents).
- REQ-V7 full voice parity.
- REQ-QA2/QA3 gates (final blockers).
- All 2.0 items.
- Using the root seam.
- PC support, haptics, bigger tap targets.

### Corpus Readiness (current)
173 PNG + 173 labels + 173 uixml (clean after fixes).  
Strong for alpha-critical: training hub (multiple states), training sub (all 5 facilities, deltas, supports), events (choices + Effects), race entry/list/gate/results, many generic modals/Close/Next. Spirit-burst GO + aftermath captured.  

Gaps (acceptable): `shop/`, `infirmary/`, `recreation/` empty; deeper pre-race/strategy, shop commit, hamburger, full Skills list. Facility-row spirit-burst badges thin for V17 (later).

Existing `tools/capture_screen.sh` + single-shot "snap" discipline already matches the passive, user-originated rule.

### What I Produced
`docs/1.0-alpha-plan.md` — self-contained public handoff doc with:
- Exact definition + gates
- Hard must-haves vs. acceptable incompletes
- Current snapshot + gaps
- Minimal component sketch (service shell, pipeline, corpus, sequences, overlay, partial voice, safety)
- Pre-scaffold checklist
- Open items that stay open

### REQ-P3 Boundary (Critical)
Implementation + APK are **closed/private**. Top-level design docs here stay public.  
**Scaffolding the Android project, first `AccessibilityService` stub, build files, or anything that produces a binary must happen in a genuinely separate private repository.** No early public skeleton. Nothing that builds the APK lands in or is exposed through this repo.

import re, json
from pathlib import Path
text = Path("REQUIREMENTS.md").read_text()
print("Scanning source for milestone references in REQs...")
for m in re.finditer(r"- \*\*(REQ-[A-Z][A-Z0-9]+)[^*]*\*\*.*?(?=\n- \*\*(?:REQ-|OQ-)|^##|\Z)", text, re.S):
    rid = m.group(1)
    body = m.group(0)[:700]
    hits = []
    if re.search(r"1\.0 alpha|alpha-critical|acceptable.*alpha|for 1\.0 alpha", body, re.I): hits.append("alpha")
    if re.search(r"1\.0 beta|beta hard|hard blocker.*beta|for 1\.0 beta", body, re.I): hits.append("beta")
    if re.search(r"1\.0 final|hard blocker.*final|for 1\.0 final", body, re.I): hits.append("final")
    if re.search(r"\b2\.0\b|provisionally.*2\.0|targeted at 2\.0|explicitly 2\.0", body, re.I): hits.append("2.0")
    if hits:
        print(rid, hits)
print("---")
html = Path("docs/requirements-map.html").read_text()
m = re.search(r"const DATA = (\{.*?\});\n\n", html, re.S)
if m:
    data = json.loads(m.group(1))
    tagged = [n for n in data.get("nodes", []) if n.get("milestones")]
    print("Nodes with milestone tags in rendered data:", len(tagged))
    for t in tagged[:12]:
        print(" ", t["id"], t.get("milestones"))
else:
    print("Could not parse DATA blob")
'  (Verify which requirements got milestone tags extracted and confirm in rendered output.)

data = json.loads(m.group(1))
by_ms = {}
for n in data["nodes"]:
    for m in n.get("milestones") or []:
        by_ms.setdefault(m, []).append(n["id"])
print("Milestone coverage in rendered map:")
for k in ["alpha","beta","final","2_0"]:
    print(" ", k, ":", len(by_ms.get(k, [])), "nodes e.g.", (by_ms.get(k) or [])[:4])
print("Total nodes with >=1 milestone tag:", len([n for n in data["nodes"] if n.get("milestones")]))

## Assistant

**Yes — implemented and regenerated.**

The rendered requirements map (`docs/index.html`, `docs/requirements-map.html`, and the root copy) now has live milestone toggles.

### What was added

**New controls in the header** (right after the section dropdown):

```
milestones: [ ] alpha  [ ] beta  [ ] final  [ ] 2.0
```

- Color-coded labels (subtle backgrounds matching the existing palette).
- Tooltip explains the behavior.

**Behavior**:
- When **no** milestone boxes are checked → everything behaves exactly as before (full set + your other filters).
- When you check one or more (e.g. just **alpha**, or **alpha + beta**):
  - Only nodes tagged for **at least one** of the selected milestones are shown (both in the tree and the graph).
  - Matching items in the tree get a gold outline (`milestone-hit`).
  - Nodes in the cytoscape graph get a distinctive double gold border.
  - Small inline badges also appear next to affected items in the tree (e.g. `alpha` `beta`).

**Extraction** is automatic and runs on every regeneration. It looks for the language already present in `REQUIREMENTS.md`:
- `1.0 alpha`, `alpha-critical`, `acceptable ... alpha`, `for 1.0 alpha`, etc.
- `1.0 beta`, `beta hard`, `hard blocker ... beta`, etc.
- `1.0 final`, `hard blocker ... final`, etc.
- `2.0`, `provisionally ... 2.0`, `targeted at 2.0`, `explicitly 2.0`, etc.

These tags are stored on the nodes themselves (`"milestones": ["alpha", "beta", ...]`), so the UI is just reflecting what's in the source document.

### Current coverage (after regeneration)

| Milestone | Examples |
|-----------|----------|
| alpha     | REQ-F5 (training sweep), REQ-V7, REQ-QA2, REQ-OQ3 |
| beta      | REQ-V7, REQ-QA2, OQ-22, REQ-OQ3 |
| final     | REQ-QA2, REQ-QA3, REQ-V7, REQ-OQ3 |
| 2.0       | REQ-R1, REQ-H1, REQ-V10, OQ-27, ... |

(10 nodes total currently carry milestone tags. The patterns will catch more as you add explicit language in the doc.)

### How to use

1. Edit `REQUIREMENTS.md` (add clearer milestone phrasing anywhere in a REQ or OQ block if you want it to light up).
2. Re-run:
   ```sh
   python3.12 tools/gen_requirements_map.py
   ```
3. Open the map (locally `docs/index.html` or the Pages URL).

The toggles work together with search, edge-type checkboxes, section filter, and "hide resolved".

Want any tweaks (different extraction heuristics, more badges in the graph, a "show only alpha-critical" preset button, etc.)?

## Assistant

**Yes, a few clear inconsistencies / tensions specific to 1.0 alpha.**

Here are the most obvious ones after a targeted pass against the current REQUIREMENTS.md (focused on alpha definition, F5 priority, milestone gates, overlay requirements, process obligations, and scope statements):

### 1. Gap-pass / architecture gate vs. current state (process inconsistency)
- OQ-32 (and the resolved note) explicitly requires a **deliberate full-document gap pass before 1.0 alpha architecture work starts in earnest**.
- OQ-1 is still tagged **BLOCKING** with the note "worth resolving before 1.0 architecture work goes further".
- The doc treats alpha as "first working build" that will include real `AccessibilityService` + `dispatchGesture` usage.
- We are now reviewing for scaffolding. The gap pass + OQ-1 resolution have not been declared complete in the document.

This is the cleanest process vs. timeline mismatch.

### 2. Always-visible overlay is required for alpha, but its testing gate is final-only
- REQ-A7, REQ-A10, and REQ-V9 make the persistent overlay kill switches (sweep + voice listening) fundamental. REQ-F5 makes sweep alpha-critical, so the overlay toggle comes along immediately.
- REQ-A7's rationale even calls out that floating strips must not obscure the game (cross-ref REQ-QA2).
- REQ-SF3 has a special carve-out that only exists "once REQ-A10/REQ-V9 exist".
- But **REQ-QA2** ("UI overlay tested against every scenario") is a **hard blocker only for 1.0 final**.

Alpha will ship the overlay controls with no documented intermediate coverage/testing bar. The SF3 clarification assumes they exist and need special handling, but nothing says what "good enough for alpha" looks like.

### 3. "In scope for 1.0" list is not alpha-differentiated
The top-level "In scope for 1.0" bullet list (and the out-of-scope counterpart) treats the whole 1.0 line as one thing:
- It lists "Audio readout of choices via on-device TTS — conditional on REQ-T4's gate, currently looking achievable for 1.0".
- Shop browse is called out in F5 as "still 1.0, after (1)/(2)".

Meanwhile the Milestones section + REQ-V7 + OQ-22 carve out many things as beta (full voice parity, shop purchase actions, etc.). The high-level scope section never splits alpha vs. beta cleanly. This makes "what exactly ships in the first working build" harder to read than it should be.

### 4. TTS is presented inconsistently for alpha
- Listed under "In scope for 1.0".
- REQ-T4 calls the entire subsection "a soft, conditional requirement, not a hard 1.0 commitment".
- The updates in REQ-T4 and REQ-M3 make it look more feasible (corpus text + no root needed), but the language still hedges it heavily.

If alpha is the "first working build," having TTS in the in-scope list while the controlling requirement says it is not a hard commitment is muddy.

### 5. Minor but real: OQ-1 remains the only pre-architecture BLOCKING item and is structurally hard to close for alpha
The doc says the gesture-detection risk "hasn't been spiked" and that the obvious cheap test is forbidden by REQ-DEV1/DEV2.  
For alpha you will be dispatching real gestures against the live client. Leaving the only remaining BLOCKING item in that state while planning to scaffold is a latent risk the document itself flags.

### Other notes (not hard inconsistencies but worth calling out)
- Shop "browse-through" (F5 + A1) is alpha, but actual purchase is part of the beta-blocking OQ-22 inventory. The distinction is made in the text, but it is easy to misread at a glance.
- REQ-A16 (list auto-scroll) is automatically pulled into alpha because it shares the A10 sweep toggle. This is probably fine, but race-list depth is still OQ-22.
- The 1.0-alpha-plan.md we just wrote is actually clearer on the alpha slice than the raw REQUIREMENTS.md in a couple of places.

**Summary of the biggest problems for alpha readiness:**
1. Process gate (OQ-32 + OQ-1 BLOCKING) not reconciled with "we are about to scaffold."
2. Overlay must exist for alpha kill switches, but the only explicit overlay testing obligation is a final blocker.
3. Scope statements are mostly written at the "1.0" level rather than alpha/beta/final.

Would you like me to propose concrete wording patches for any of these (especially the gap-pass timing, the overlay expectation for alpha, or tightening the in-scope list)? Or should we focus on one area first?

**REQ-QA2 (full text):**

1. **F5 / "still 1.0" language for shop browse** (biggest leftover nit)
   - REQ-F5 still says:

   - With the new alpha restriction ("only the Aoharu Hai career loop itself"), shop browse is now effectively optional/stretch for alpha, not a required 1.0 alpha sequence. The wording doesn't reflect the new carve-out.

2. **Broad "In scope for 1.0" list sits right above the restriction**
   - The general list is still written at the full-1.0 level. The new restriction subsection fixes it in practice, but a reader can still get the impression that everything listed above is alpha scope until they read the next paragraph.

   - One place says "Nothing on the main menu..." while another says "main menu (except the ability to exit...)". Minor, but slightly inconsistent phrasing.

4. **Everything else**
   - OQ-22 / beta blockers (shop purchase, full Skills, full Races, etc.) remain correctly beta-only.
   - Exit is handled.
   - No new contradictions introduced with overlays, safety rules, or REQ-P3.

## Assistant

**1.0 alpha is narrowly scoped to the Aoharu Hai (Unity Cup) in-career loop once a run is already in progress.**

Only required out-of-career capability: clean exit (Save & Exit or Give Up) + stop the assist.  
Nothing on main menu, pre-career, lobby, support selection, or non-Aoharu content.

### Hard Must-Haves (alpha-critical)

**Sequence priority (REQ-F5):**
1. **Training auto-sweep** (REQ-A9 + A10) — hover Speed/Stamina/Power/Guts/Wit with configurable dwell (~1.5s default). Same toggle arms list auto-scroll (REQ-A16, race list primary).
2. **No-choice auto-advance** (REQ-F2 + F4) — dialogue, results, skips, "Next"/"Close" etc. via human-labeled generic-UI corpus entries. Ships in parallel with sweep as labels exist.
3. **Shop browse-through** (REQ-A1) — lower priority; still 1.0 but after the first two work.

**Core mechanisms (day one):**
- `AccessibilityService` (API 30+): `takeScreenshot()`, `dispatchGesture()`, foreground notification, package guard (`com.cygames.umamusume` only).
- Screen understanding: ML Kit bundled OCR + fuzzy match against offline corpus (event text from local `master.mdb` extract + generic-UI layer).
- Corpus with human "no-choice" vs "has-choice" labels (REQ-F4/M5/M6).
- Named sequences only (REQ-A1) — no general gameplay loop.
- Decision replay (REQ-A4 + A8): per-event/support-card stored selections; first occurrence never auto-fires; global + per-event toggles.
- Always-visible overlay kill switches (REQ-A7 + A10 + V9) — **alpha bar** items:
  - Sweep toggle (covers training sweep + list auto-scroll).
  - Voice listening toggle.
  - Proper self-exclusion under REQ-SF3 so overlays don't block all game actions.
- Safety/ethics: no self-loop (A5), never faster than best human (A6), no network (S1), SF1/SF3/SF6, DEV1-DEV3 (user-present, single-button, two-step, uninstall after spikes).
- Voice basics (partial OK per REQ-V7): heed wakeword + Vosk, core commands (facilities + "energy", rest, recreation/"date", ordinals, on-screen text, gamble/safe/take-the-energy, race/scheduled, start/stop listening), confirmation window, TTS readout where corpus text is available.

**REQ-P3 boundary:** All APK-producing code (project, manifest, service subclass, etc.) must live in a separate private repo. Public repo stays docs + passive tools + screenshots only.

### Explicitly Not Required for Alpha
- Full voice control of everything inside a career (REQ-V7) — partial is fine.
- Complete OQ-22 inventory (shop purchase, skills purchase, full races beyond list+scheduled+entry, recreation, infirmary, hamburger).
- REQ-QA1 (human-verified UI coverage) — beta.
- REQ-QA2 (overlay tested against every scenario, including Twinkle URA Finals + Grand Live) + REQ-QA3 (security audit) — final.
- REQ-VAL1/REQ-VAL3 (human/manual requirements validation pass) — final.
- 2.0 items, root fallback usage, PC support, haptics, bigger targets, etc.

### Current State (as of plan)
- 173/173 PNG + `.labels.txt` + `.uixml` (balanced).
- Strong alpha coverage: training hub + all 5 facilities (multiple states + stat deltas + failure %), events/choices, races (list, scheduled, details, gate, results, post-race), spirit burst (GO + aftermath), generic UI modals, menu (Save & Exit / Give Up visible).
- Acceptable gaps: `shop/`, `infirmary/`, `recreation/` empty; some deeper race/strategy/result variants.

## Assistant

**Done.**

The requirement was added under **REQ-A4** (with a supporting cross-reference in REQ-V15) and reflected in the alpha plan.

### Exact addition (REQ-A4)

```markdown
- **Any deliberate user selection of an option at a recognized decision
  point sets or updates the recorded selection for future replay
  (REQ-A4).** This applies equally whether the user selects by directly
  tapping the on-screen option button or by issuing a voice command that
  names the option (REQ-V15 and related forms). Both input methods
  establish the precedent: "last time this exact (support card, event)
  appeared, the user chose this concrete option." The stored value is
  the concrete option identity, not the input channel used to pick it.
  This symmetry ensures that manual tapping and voice selection are
  treated identically for the purpose of building a standing recorded
  answer.
```

### Reinforcement in REQ-V15 (on-screen text form)

> Selecting via voice in this way sets or updates the recorded selection (REQ-A4) exactly as directly tapping the same on-screen option would — the input method does not matter for precedent.

### Alpha plan update

In the Decision replay section:

> Direct tap on an option and voice selection of the same option are equivalent for setting/updating the recorded precedent.

### Other effects
- Requirements map regenerated.
- The change is scoped to recognized decision points (the same ones REQ-A4 already covers). First occurrence still always falls through to the user. The stored value remains the concrete option identity.

The precedent rule is now symmetric by design. Let me know if you want wording tweaks, an explicit OQ, or anything else.

## Assistant

Done.

- REQ-V4 now states the general rule up front: **double utterance is required for any selection whose normal touch path is tap-once-then-tap (or tap-then-confirm-in-dialog)**. Applies uniformly to all such two-step affordances.
- REQ-V12 title + lead paragraph rewritten as the upward generalization: "Double utterance for any selection that must be tapped twice or tapped then confirmed in a dialog." First utterance arms/selects; second (repeat or synonym) commits. Not limited to consequential actions.
- Race-grade doubles, other race pointers, training commits, event options, and burst picks updated to reference the general rule (REQ-V12) instead of "same pattern as" or "double form".
- Wording cleaned: "double utterance" preferred over "double form"; "select-then-double-commit" replaces "select-vs-double-begin".
- Map regenerated (`python3.12 tools/gen_requirements_map.py`).

Key excerpts after edit:

**REQ-V4:**
> - **Double utterance is the general rule for any double-tap or tap-then-confirm selection.** See REQ-V12. Any on-screen selection whose normal touch path is "tap once to select/focus/preview, then tap again (or tap a confirm in a dialog) to commit" must be performed with two utterances under the same pattern...

**REQ-V12:**
> - **REQ-V12 — Double utterance for any selection that must be tapped twice or tapped then confirmed in a dialog.** This is the general upward rule covering every on-screen selection whose normal physical path is a two-step interaction...

Ready for next capture or doc pass.

- Private git repo (no code ever touched the public repo)
- Standard Android project structure (minSdk 30, target 34)
- `AccessibilityService` declared + config (REQ-M1, REQ-PL4)
- Hard package guard: **only acts on `com.cygames.umamusume` when it is foreground**
- Core alpha entry points:
  - `performTrainingSweepOnce()` — REQ-A9 / REQ-F5 (training auto-sweep)
  - `performNoChoiceAdvance()` — REQ-F2 / REQ-F4
  - `performCareerExit()` — exit path (Save & Exit / Give Up)
- Kill switches (REQ-A7/A10/V9):
  - `sweepEnabled`
  - `voiceEnabled`
  - Immediate effect via `isInUma` guard
- No `INTERNET` permission, no network code (REQ-S1)
- ML Kit Text Recognition dependency (REQ-M4)
- Minimal overlay-style MainActivity with toggles
- Never self-loop design (REQ-A5) — every action requires an explicit call

// Spoof os.name to Linux *before* pluginManagement so AGP resolves Linux aapt2/build-tools.
// Real tools are brandelf'ed by scripts/prepare-freebsd-build.sh so the Linuxulator executes them.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# take-snapshot.sh
# Run from inside the PUBLIC repo (UMAssisted) after reasonable progress in the private checkout.
# This creates/updates the snapshot under private/1.0-alpha/ and stages it for commit+push.

set -euo pipefail

PRIVATE_CHECKOUT="${PRIVATE_CHECKOUT:-/home/dev/UMAssisted-private}"
SNAPSHOT_DIR="private/1.0-alpha"

if [[ ! -d "$PRIVATE_CHECKOUT" ]]; then
  echo "ERROR: Private checkout not found at $PRIVATE_CHECKOUT" >&2
  echo "Set PRIVATE_CHECKOUT=/path/to/private if different." >&2
  exit 1
fi

echo "==> Snapshotting from $PRIVATE_CHECKOUT into $SNAPSHOT_DIR"

TS=$(date -u +"%Y%m%d_%H%M%SZ")
mkdir -p "$SNAPSHOT_DIR"/{src,artifacts}

# Clean source snapshot (exclude heavy transient stuff)
( cd "$PRIVATE_CHECKOUT" && \
  tar --exclude='.git' \
      --exclude='.gradle' \
      --exclude='build' \
      --exclude='*.iml' \
      --exclude='local.properties' \
      --exclude='.kotlin' \
      -cf - . ) | ( cd "$SNAPSHOT_DIR/src" && rm -rf ./* 2>/dev/null || true; tar -xf - )

# APK (latest debug for now)
APK_SRC="$PRIVATE_CHECKOUT/app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk"
if [[ -f "$APK_SRC" ]]; then
  cp "$APK_SRC" "$SNAPSHOT_DIR/artifacts/app-debug.apk"
  cp "$APK_SRC" "$SNAPSHOT_DIR/artifacts/UMAssisted-1.0-alpha-$TS.apk"
  echo "    APK copied: $(ls -lh "$SNAPSHOT_DIR/artifacts/app-debug.apk" | awk '{print $5}')"
else
  echo "    (no debug APK found at $APK_SRC — skipping)"
fi

# Update PROGRESS.md with latest info
PRIVATE_COMMIT=$(cd "$PRIVATE_CHECKOUT" && git log --oneline -1 2>/dev/null || echo "unknown")
cat > "$SNAPSHOT_DIR/PROGRESS.md" << EOP
# 1.0 Alpha Build Progress Snapshot

**Snapshot time (UTC):** $(date -u +"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")  
**Private repo commit:** $PRIVATE_COMMIT  
**Public repo snapshot location:** private/1.0-alpha/

## Artifacts
- artifacts/app-debug.apk (latest)
- artifacts/UMAssisted-1.0-alpha-$TS.apk (timestamped)

## How this was produced
Run from public repo root:
  PRIVATE_CHECKOUT=/path/to/private ./private/1.0-alpha/take-snapshot.sh
Then:
  git add private/1.0-alpha
  git commit -m "private: 1.0 alpha build progress $(date -u +%Y-%m-%d)"
  git push

See README.md for full process and scope notes.
EOP

echo "==> Snapshot complete at $SNAPSHOT_DIR"
echo "Next: git add private/1.0-alpha && git commit && git push"
EOS

## Assistant

**Done.**

The requirement has been added (primarily as an extension to **REQ-A14**, with supporting updates to **REQ-V15**, **OQ-38**, the 1.0 beta milestone language, and the plan doc + regenerated map).

### Summary of the change

**REQ-A14** now explicitly recognizes two layouts for semantic event-option commands:

- **Classic gamble/safe layout** (one or more options have multiple possible outcomes; others have a single fixed outcome) — unchanged core rules for `"gamble"` / `"safe"`.
- **Pure-gamble / all-identical-effects layout** (new): the Effects / Choices dialog (or offline corpus label) shows that **every option produces identical outcomes** — the visible choice text is cosmetic; any option has exactly the same effect as any other.

In the pure-gamble case:
- `"gamble"`, `"anything"`, and `"whatever"` (plus user-defined synonyms under REQ-V11) are valid commands.
- They select the **first** option (top-most in the presented list / Effects order).
- Detection prefers the live Effects/Choices readout (via the existing REQ-A15a automation path) when offline tags are insufficient; the corpus may also pre-mark the event.
- `"safe"` does not apply (no distinguished non-gamble option exists).

Validity rules, fall-through behavior, failure messages, recording (REQ-A4/A8), and TTS guidance were updated accordingly. The requirement stays user-originated only (no auto-pick policy).

**Other updates**
- REQ-V15 semantic role forms now list the identical-effects case alongside the classic gamble/safe and energy forms.
- OQ-38 updated to note that the pure-gamble / all-identical case + the three command forms are now specified; remaining exotic mixed-layout cataloging is still open.
- Minor cross-references in the 1.0 beta definition and docs/1.0-alpha-plan.md.
- Map regenerated (`docs/index.html`, `docs/requirements-map.html`, `requirements-map.html`).

The behavior is deliberately narrow and safe: it only fires on explicit user command (or a recorded precedent that happened to be the first option), requires evidence of identical effects (Effects dialog or corpus), and falls through cleanly otherwise — consistent with REQ-A11 / REQ-A5 / REQ-M3.

All changes are in `REQUIREMENTS.md` (plus the regenerated map and plan doc).

## Assistant

**Done.**

Added **REQ-A15b** ("Optional timed auto-open of the Effects dialog") immediately after the existing REQ-A15a section.

### What the requirement says (summary)

- The user can enable a setting (in-career scoped, off by default) that causes UMAssisted to automatically run the existing "open event effects" named sequence (REQ-A15a) after a **user-configurable delay** when a recognized has-choice option selection screen appears.
- The delay is wall-clock time (sensible starting default goes into the OQ-33 calibration bucket). The timer lets the user read the visible choice text first.
- **Information only** — it opens the Effects / Choices panel (or equivalent) for readout. It **never** selects an option. The user must still issue an explicit command ("first", "gamble", "take the energy", on-screen text, etc.) or rely on a recorded precedent (REQ-A4/A8).
- Strictly optional, exposed in the control surface/settings, always subject to the global kill switches.
- Limited to recognized choice/option-selection screens inside supported careers.
- Cancellable: any user input (touch or voice) before the timer fires cancels it for that screen.
- Non-blocking on failure (wrong state, overlay, etc.).
- Reuses the exact same named accessibility sequence and safety rules as explicit Effects automation (REQ-A1, REQ-A5, REQ-A11, REQ-SF3).
- Traced as its own ID (REQ-A15b) for clarity; it is a convenience layer on top of REQ-A15a.

### Other updates made for consistency

- Mentioned REQ-A15b in the Effects automation paragraph (the "when that sequence is needed for..." list).
- Added the new delay to the **OQ-33 calibration bucket**.
- Regenerated the interactive requirements map (`docs/index.html`, `docs/requirements-map.html`, `requirements-map.html`, and root copy).

The change is purely additive, stays inside the existing safety/scope discipline, and does not alter what constitutes a selection or expand automation authority.
